<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which statement best describes this protein’s induction under starvation conditions?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Its expression is induced rapidly after starvation begins.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Its expression becomes induced only after prolonged starvation.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Its expression is not induced under starvation conditions.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Its expression is induced specifically when starvation halts growth.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Step 1: Identify the protein.**<br>The sequence shows key structural features:<br>- A Walker A/P-loop motif (&quot;ILATKGPSST&quot;) — a hallmark of nucleotide-binding domains<br>- Multiple hydrophobic segments at the C-terminus consistent with transmembrane helices<br>- Overall domain architecture resembling a dehydrogenase/b-heme cytochrome fusion, characteristic of NADPH oxidase (NOX/RBOH) family members or related membrane-bound oxidoreductases<br><br>**Step 2: Biological context of starvation.**<br>In starvation biology (particularly well-studied in yeast), there is a critical distinction between:<br>- *Nutrient limitation*: slows growth but doesn&#x27;t necessarily halt it<br>- *Growth arrest*: the actual cessation of proliferation, which triggers a distinct physiological program (entry into quiescence)<br><br>**Step 3: Evaluate each option.**<br>- **A** (rapid induction after starvation begins): This would apply to early stress-response proteins (e.g., heat shock factors). The oxidoreductase-like function doesn&#x27;t fit a &quot;rapid first-wave&quot; response.<br>- **B** (induced only after prolonged starvation): This is time-dependent induction, which is a different regulatory logic than growth-arrest-coupled induction.<br>- **C** (not induced under starvation): The protein&#x27;s structural features (NADPH-binding, membrane localization) suggest a functional role in metabolic remodeling, making constitutive-only expression unlikely in a starvation context.<br>- **D** (induced specifically when starvation halts growth): This aligns with proteins whose expression is coupled to the *event* of growth arrest rather than to time or nutrient concentration alone. <td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> When assessing a protein discussed in the context of stress-response regulation, distinguish effects on downstream gene expression from changes in the protein’s own abundance.
